ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e5536	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Today's debate is not a mere or cheap debate, but rather is based on the deep concern about what has been placed on the agenda of this House today and is based on the concern about our Austrian constitutional principles, which is ultimately based on the concern that the freedom and sovereignty of Austria and also our budgetary sovereignty are at risk. [[Abg. Dr. Bartenstein: Well, let's see!]] [[Ah-Rufe at the SPÖ]] And I say, unlike SPÖ, ÖVP and the Greens, we do not want to serve the big banks or speculators, but we have to serve a group, namely the Austrian population and their interests. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] That would be the task of every individual parliamentarian here: to serve the Austrian population. We call for the removal of the parts of the agenda concerning the decision-making of the ESM, the so-called European Stability Mechanism, from the agenda of today's plenary session, because, in our view, a binding referendum would be necessary on this crucial issue and you would refuse it. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] De facto would be the ESM, the European Stability Mechanism, namely the transfer of Austrian sovereign rights towards an empowerment law, a dictatorial authority, an executive body called the Board of Governors within the ESM Board of Governors, which will ultimately be able to operate without control by the legislative or judicial authorities. And these are fundamental changes that we believe can only be enforceable with a binding referendum. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] This authorisation - and this is an empowerment law - through a treaty ultimately means that the own competence, which constitutes the independence of a state, namely the parliamentary budgetary sovereignty, should be abandoned in the future, and one will be completely unimpeded by this Parliament in the future determined from outside, what must be done with the Austrian taxpayer's money. And this is a serious intervention into the Constitution and therefore, in our opinion, of course, we must have a referendum. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] If today red, black and green, these three parties, decide solely to participate in an ESM, then this is nothing else – and one must call it so – as a constitutional coup or also a cold coup, whose goal de facto is to abolish the Second Republic in favour of Austria's rise in a centralist European state structure, as already defined by Mr Spindelegger. [[He- and Hello-Rufe]] [[Admissibility at the FPÖ]] Finally, it must be said that the ESM Board of Governors, which is not controlled democratically, which does not have to account for the executive, the judicial, the parliaments, of course, is an instrument of a financial dictatorship, otherwise it cannot be described. And the individual Member States of the European Union would have – if it were to achieve this objective, which has become apparent, which apparently has three parties in this House, namely, to drive forward decisions towards a centralist European state that should no longer be reversed – and then we, as part of the European Union, would have fewer rights than individual American states in the event of a catastrophic implementation. This is a way towards an authoritarian system, which is a reflection of basic democratic principles. And only the Sovereign can decide on this serious step in an unwholesome but also very distressing future, and the Sovereign is now the Austrian population. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] It cannot be that you want to take the sovereign's right to have his money, that is, tax money, so that the Austrian Sovereign through you today – by three parties – should be forced to pump his tax money into marode banking systems and pleite states for which he has no responsibility. [[President Mag. Prammer gives the bell sign]] These are irresponsible processes, where the Austrian people also have the right to secure a binding referendum.

The same applies to the question – that would be the next paragraph – that loans to Spain, which are currently being discussed, will first be granted by the EFSF, then, so to speak, to the ESM, “with gaining seniority status”. That's also a delicate point. Under the current ESM Treaty, ESM’s “seniority status” criteria have been applied. But this is not an actual part of the contract, it is point 13 of the Preamble. So whether this is a change in the Treaty or whether a decision by the Heads of Government is sufficient here is to be examined. Once again, Parliament’s “bees chambers” will have to deal with this. And much more is the case for the third – and rightly the most eye-catching – paragraph, namely the question of whether the ESM will not be able to provide assistance without the use of the troika without the relatively complicated procedure, which is currently intended for financial assistance, for the use of the ESM's financial instruments. The main focus is on primary market interventions or secondary market interventions. This paragraph was interpreted by the media as a victory of Mario Monti over Chancellor Merkel. One can debate this. I am not a priority against these changes. I am just saying that Article 13 of the ESM Treaty, on which we are voting today, does not provide for this possibility. If, in future, Italy or another country can, under certain conditions, comply with the six-pack, comply with all the previous provisions, as planned here, for example, under simplified conditions, under secondary market interventions, I believe that Article 13 must be amended to take account of the procedural provisions currently in force. In other words, Minister Fekter has to debate this in the Board of Governors, Parliament’s “Bekenkammers” have to deal with it, and Minister Fekter has to give a yes or no to the crucial meeting. By the way, ladies and gentlemen of FPÖ and BZÖ: In the future, Parliament's "bee-rooms" will have rights which they did not have until now, because if that were all about the EFSF, we would have nothing to say here in Parliament, no matter which "bee-chamber" we are talking about. This changes significantly with the ESM Treaty. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e12762	Mr President. High house! When it comes to the ESM, from my point of view, we have to solve some language confusion. That's what it's all about! Now I ask you: If a country like Greece, Spain or Italy does not receive new money from the financial markets, then it will no longer be able to accept new debt at reasonable interest rates. – Is this due to the bad financial markets that individual countries shoot out and then punish them with horrendous interest, 8, 9, 10 percent, for whatever reason? Is that really the case? That means that the problem is not that those countries are cut off from cheap loans, but the problem is that those countries have not understood that high interest rates always mean a political failure. This is evident in the history of financial disasters, and we already had many. If you look back 600 years: We had very, very many financial disasters. Finally, a political failure has always been the reason for the interest rates being exhausted, because everyone who gives money to a state is interested in getting the money back! If that's in danger, you either don't get any money, or you just have to pay a risk supplement accordingly. That's exactly what we're experiencing. If we deal with this ESM now, artificially reduce this risk supplement to zero and allow those countries to return to cheap debt, after what do you think, then will happen? – It will happen exactly what has always happened in the past: Those countries simply don't have any reform pressure anymore! Then they would not have received money from the financial markets for some time, and they should have come out with their own money. That's the point! In that case, we say to each other that as European states, we are in solidarity with each other. If only a person gets interest in rage that pushes him into ruin, then he does not need to borrow the money there, but then we put together and together borrow the money from that country – at reasonable, at quiet interest rates, but also at strict conditions, under strict control. Internal stability is about how high inflation is. The inflation since the introduction of the euro is about half as high as it was before the introduction of the euro. It is a protective wall against speculation – the speculation that is happening against individual EU countries – and will also in future make it impossible to speculate as easily as ever. The purpose of the ESM is to mobilise funds for Member States with severe financing problems, but not simply to mobilise them, but to make them available under conditions. This has probably created a lasting, improved protection against systemic crises. This is not only an important point for the problem states, but also for Austria, because these systemic crises can also have a negative impact on Austria in the medium term. At the same time, however, he also said in his report that the greatest danger for Austria is the non-existent protection against systemic crises. But Austria's contribution is not disproportionately large either, to say that clearly: 2.8 percent is our contribution in the ESM. This corresponds to the Austrian performance and performance of the Austrian economy. The risk is ultimately limited to the share of capital; a construction similar to that of a GesmbH. Not to be forgotten is also: The ESM is a crisis instrument, it has been created as an answer to the crisis, but of course it is not the only answer to the crisis. We also need growth and employment, we need fiscal consolidation, we need regulation of financial markets, and we ultimately need a fair distribution of assets and economic activity within Europe and within the individual states. ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e19371	We're now on the 7th. Agenda item. The debate opens up to Mr. Dr. Fichtenbauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e19395	Mr. Federal Chancellor! Mr. State Secretary! Mr President. High house! Apart from the debate that is now taking place in business order, we find that the ten-year-old missing Wiener Kind Sebastian has been found dead by the distributed newspapers. I would like to express my condolences to his parents and to this example how life plays, how close together real drama is and what we feel as a drama when we throw things at each other's head. In view of what is happening away from this House, we should occasionally consider our own actions to be relative. Apart from that, we are now talking about the serious issue that we have already voted on, and about the Fiscal Pact. I present what I have already said in the Constitutional Committee, and I give the following critical light. The critical points on the Fiscal Pact are that only the Member States have a right of action at the ECJ, but not the Commission or other EU institutions. It is politically extremely unlikely that one Member State may sue another Member State in breach of the Treaty provisions of the EU tax pact – serious errors of the classification. Moreover, the ECJ does not, on its own, have the slightest documents which could prove the infringement of Treaty provisions by a Member State, in particular the insufficient implementation of the strict fiscal rules in national law and compliance with them. The Treaty does not regulate the way the ECJ would come to its documents in the case of proceedings. In any event, it would be useful for the European Court of Auditors to carry out and report on such investigations half-yearly as an early warning system. Moreover, it does not appear to be very effective to impose fines on Member States that violate the Fiscal Pact and do not have any money. They would have to take them out of the cover of the ESM, which is an extremely contradictory law. The constitutional problem, negated by the supporters of the Fiscal Pact, has already been discussed at length. In particular, these are restrictions on Austrian budgetary sovereignty and the problem of the Austrian upper institution, namely the Minister for Finance and Finance, being bound to the vote of the Commission, i.e. a binding relationship between the the Austrian democratically legitimised body and another non-democraticly legitimate body – extremely problematic. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] As an indication of how critically and not jubilantly reported on the part of public-law broadcasting in other countries, I invite you to look at the ARD homepage, where the following headlines are clearly given by Klaus-Rainer Jackisch in the column ‘Euroschau’: “The Euro project has failed. Merkel threw their principles overboard. Policy has decided to save the euro – it costs what it wants. The Federal Chancellor threw her principles overboard." The same is true for Austria as well. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] It also says: “Parliament is being decomposed”, “United Kingdom is considering the unthinkable”, namely leaving the EU. Both variants, saving the euro or placing it on a new currency would be an invaluable cost in both systems. For the experts we had here, it was clear that the variant of saving the euro through a new currency for healthy countries would tend to be the cheaper model. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In this context, I would ask you to understand that we cannot give this project an agreement.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e19478	Next speaker: Mr Krainer. - Please.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e20148	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Arbitrator. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e20170	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Perhaps right to my colleague. Scheibner, because he's giving me a headshake – I can explain it to you –: The interest rate of Eurobonds or Community European bonds is not a mixed rate, as you would say, not an average rate, as it is mathematically called – mathematically weighted – of national interest rates, but it is a new offer to the market and therefore also depending on who makes this offer, and the size of the offeror determines the interest rate. And since this offeror is then among the largest in the world, there is also a strong evidence in literature that the interest rate for all parties involved, even if the trend is going down, does not go so much up. [[Abg. Scheibner: That's what we'll look at!]] But back to the ESM and the Fiscal Pact to deal with today's issue. We have already discussed the ESM this morning; all of these things are related because they represent the answers of Europe to the various forms of the economic crisis. The Fiscal Pact aims to improve the coordination of the European States and the budgetary policies of the European States, to restore Europe's stability zone, which also restores market confidence. That is exactly what the Fiscal Pact was created for. It has a wide range of objectives and measures, including the better coordination of individual government bonds and lending in the markets, and the pre-conception of the European Commission in the European Semester with a view to commenting and the like. Of course, more budgetary discipline is also to be achieved. The two main points are to first reduce the structural deficit in the medium term towards zero and to produce second permanently balanced government budgets. You have to say two things consciously, I think, if you look at these points more closely. The structural deficit is the deficit adjusted for the economic effects. In other words, the economic downturn costs would be calculated from the structural deficit and make it possible for economic fluctuations and thus also the automatic stabilizers, as it is so beautiful in the German economy, to work. The cost of social security, namely that people receive unemployment compensation, that a reasonable labour market policy is made, that there is social security, health insurance, and, and, of course, would also rise in the crisis, but would continue to be possible. In other words, social security in a country is not in danger by defining that balanced budget. On the contrary! It is even additional in Article 3(1) lit. c states that additional fiscal impulses to boost the economy are possible in times of crisis. It is very important to stress this, because I am very sure that we are in favour of fiscal and budgetary discipline, but I am not in favour of being able to counter it again in the future in a crisis. It is also ensured with this fiscal package that, even in future crises, which we hope will not, we will be able to counter them so successfully. That is why I am saying that because I consider the reduction of deficits to be, of course, a reasonable measure, because it also reduces the dependence of the state on the financial markets and thus guarantees a viable and independent state in the medium term. The crucial question is – and this is what we must ask ourselves – whether the Fiscal Pact will continue to allow us to determine ourselves or the Austrian National Council, how we consolidate our budget and how we shape our budgetary policy. And this is exactly how it is possible to continue. It is also possible in the Fiscal Pact to seek a balanced mix of revenue-side and expenditure-side measures, between economic-invigorating measures and savings measures, between structural measures and tax measures in those areas where they do not have any growth effects, as we have already done successfully in Austria. It is very important to stress this because the discussion has now come in addition: Invest and consolidate, are they contradictions or not? – And I say quite honestly: It's no contradiction. On the contrary! Without growth, the objective of a balanced budget cannot be achieved mathematically. And that is why it is also necessary, especially when we talk about a fiscal pact and a strengthened regulatory work to create trust and budgetary discipline, that we also talk about how we create growth. That is why there is also the pact for growth and employment which the European Council has decided. 120 billion euros as a package of measures, and if we add it to the financial transaction tax which has also been set up on this Council for those states that join this coalition of willing people, then this is already a massive package. You can always discuss: Would it have been more possible or not? We know that Europe is ultimately a decision of 27 or 17 within the euro area. I am therefore also saying: It is right that we should train both legs wisely, namely the leg of trust, consolidation, financial and budgetary stability and the leg of economic growth and the creation of employment. With exactly these two legs it is possible to move forward as well. Which leg is preferable to you, is different according to the political decision. In the end, however, it makes both sense and good for Europe. That is why I think it is also right that today we have not only the ESM as a shield, but also the Fiscal Pact for greater confidence in Europe and the Growth Pact for the creation of employment and economic growth, which has, in total, produced a reasonable package. For a long time, it seemed as if Europe were moving forward unilaterally. Fortunately, Europe has been progressing reasonably for a few weeks, including economic growth and employment initiatives, which ultimately only enables fiscal consolidation.

I do not want to see this morning due to a new currency in Italy the entire tourist destinations are 40 percent cheaper and then we are no longer competitive, for example in Carinthia, but also in Styria and overall in Austria. So I think it's the wrong way to allow reorganisations about the depreciations of the southern states. [[Abg. I therefore recognise the way we have said, yes, we have to use resources to stabilise these financial markets – quite clearly in Article 3 – if that is absolutely essential. [[Abg. Grosz: We are now at the Fiscal Pact, not the ESM!]] I am very glad that we have regulated clear democratic co-determination rights through the accompanying novels. I think it is a matter of course that, when we use funds, we demand from all states that there be budgetary discipline, because that is essential in a community. There are, of course, different views on how to achieve the objectives – less than 3 percent and less than 60 percent – in principle. Today, Hollande has clearly announced that it wants to consolidate 7.5 billion revenue on its side and only 1.5 billion on its side of expenditure. That is to say, it is in the autonomy of the States whether they consolidate expenditure on the part of or on the part of revenue. I think we in Austria have taken a very balanced path. Only: If States do not comply with the rules when we have to pay money for Greece, for Spain, then to say that a fiscal pact is not necessary, we do not need to comply with the rules, which do not have to run the risk of being condemned by the ECJ, I do not understand that at all. [[Abg. Neubauer: But are constitutional!]] [[Abg. Grosz: This has already worked with the Maastricht criteria “super”!]] Of course, it's also clear: We too have to comply with the rules. Kogler: It's not all good what's a rule!]] I tell you, the ESM Treaty clearly regulates – it is important for me to clarify this at last – that the liability of each ESM member is limited with the standard deposit. We have clear tests. We have set out in the ESM accompanying innovation that if stability aid is granted, it must be decided by the individual parliaments, even if the parent capital is changed and if non-disbursed stock capital is retrieved. Then we must always see these points in the context of the Fiscal Pact, because of course it is important that we have rules to promote budgetary discipline and strengthen coordination of economic policies, because we are a monetary area, want to remain a monetary area and because this is our way. That's why I'm saying: Stability, clear democratic control, but also budgetary discipline in the sense of a successful and sustainable Europe. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e21580	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! High house! And above all, dear guests from Croatia! I would like to welcome you here in Parliament and we are very pleased that you are attending this debate. [[General condolences.]] If Croatia joins the European Union in July 2013, this will be a great success for the people in Croatia who have worked hard and have taken a long but impressive path. The way to independence for Croatia ended in 1991 in the destruction of the country. The four - year, bloody war was paid for with over 10,000 dead and over 200,000 displaced. What followed is an impressive political, social and economic development that has led Croatia step by step to the EU. Within a very short time, Croatia has turned its policies, economics and society: from war to democracy, to rule of law and to peace. This has required a great willingness for people in Croatia to change, and this deserves our respect and respect, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulation with SPÖ and ÖVP as well as the Abg. Dr. Van der Bellen.]] Now, despite all the successes, there are still some problems, especially in the field of justice, which need to be addressed. But I think that there will be decisive improvements here until accession in July 2013. With the extradition of Croatian war criminals to the International Criminal Court, Croatia has at least been able to remove a decisive obstacle to entry. Ladies and gentlemen! Croatia is today a functioning democratic rule of law with an agil market economy. This has also made Croatia a stabilising factor in the Balkans. But the EU also has a large share of this development. Croatia is an important example of the central importance of the EU as a peace and security community in Europe. And with the accession of Croatia, we are coming a great step closer to a stable and peaceful Balkans. Accession is also a positive signal for the other countries of the Western Balkans for their own European perspective. Austria will benefit to a very special extent from Croatia's accession. We are closely connected with Croatia, geographically, historically, economically and also through the large number of Croatians living here in Austria. Croatia's accession is also an economic and social gain for Austria. It is already an important trading partner for our country, and EU accession will open up further great opportunities for cooperation. This creates and secures jobs in both countries and is therefore good for Austria and Croatia. And I am also sure that cultural and social exchanges will also intensify for the benefit of both countries. I am therefore pleased to welcome Croatian citizens in the EU in July 2013. Thank you and all the best our guests from Croatia!

Kickl: We'll explain it then!]] First of all, we are disturbed by the process of holding as few votes as possible of this kind as possible on important treaties, because they are misguided and say that the chaos would break out if there were to be a negative vote in five or six countries on the possible lack of initiatives of the European Union. And if a referendum has to be held in a country, and it goes beyond that, then it will be voted on for so long until sometime it will be positive. One makes a little pressure, threatens a little, makes some concessions until all are satisfied and a good time is caught, and then it's positive and then everyone is happy again. We have faced this system with a different system. This morning we also discussed the need to talk about what the future of the Union should look like. What should this common Europe look like, which works better than the one before? Even where more and more member countries are being involved and where the decision-making process will become increasingly difficult. That is why we have said that we want and need a Europe of different speeds, with a core Europe within the European Union, where the population of the individual nation states can decide for themselves how far the degree of integration of the country should work. If you want the whole package, you will agree with it, and there it will come into force. Where this is not the case, countries should only use parts of it for themselves. It can't be that a negative vote in one country makes a potentially important project impossible for everyone else, but on the other hand, it can't be that one then picks out such special solutions so that it works overall. That is the wrong way, and that is why we do not agree with this procedure and say once again: We need a new Europe, another Europe, where the people also decide what degree of integration they want for themselves.

The present Additional Protocol to the Lisbon Treaty provides for a political commitment to Ireland following the negative outcome of the first Irish referendum on the Lisbon Treaty. I can remember that at the time 53.4 percent of Irish voted against this treaty. This Protocol takes account of the concerns of the Irish people and does not, therefore, make any changes to the content of the Treaty. It is rather a question of clarification. I can add some of these clarifications. It is about protecting the right to life, protecting the family and protecting the rights of education. The question is that the Treaty of Lisbon does not introduce new competences in the field of tax policy, and Ireland is above all concerned with its traditional military neutrality policy. Ladies and gentlemen, the minutes, provided they reach 30. In June 2013, this instrument of ratification is to enter into force at this time. The EIA agrees with this protocol. ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e24538	Next speaker: Mr Spindelberger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e24560	High house! Since we are already dealing with European issues all day long, of course I am continuing with the agreement on social security between Austria and Serbia. I would also like to try to explain why we consider such agreements necessary. Because it is not as simple as it is always described in the public that people work for a lifetime only in a state, so that they may then be able to enjoy retirement there. Rather, the opposite is the case. People have a job in one country, perhaps only for a few years, and then move to another. And then there are different social systems, where the case could occur at any time, that they can also suffer disadvantages due to their diversity. In order to illustrate this, I refer to the topic of pensions. In Austria, you only have a pension claim, if you have 15 years of contribution, i.e. 15 years of insurance. Now, however, there are a large number of people who work in several countries, but in the individual countries, there are often too few insurance periods in order to be able to obtain a pension at all. This agreement, which is incomprehensiblely rejected by some, is the very case where the insurance periods acquired in these countries can also be aggregated in future. In other words, because of such agreements, someone who has only worked for five years in Austria but has ten years of insurance in another country has a pension claim, logically only for the five years acquired here. Of course, if such agreements are not concluded, there is also a great danger in terms of double insurance. Because if we do not make such agreements, the employer may also have to pay social security contributions in Austria and in another country for the respective employees. The most extreme case, however, would be that no state in the field of social security feels responsible. Then, worst of all, it could happen that I can no longer benefit from medical services in my holiday country. So that's not what I'm saying. Such agreements, as we are going to conclude today with Serbia, are important.

Therefore, there have been no substantial, real objections either. A problem, however, has occurred clearly. The environmental impact assessment is an instrument of administrative simplification for the investor. If an investment is very large, then at the end of the day, it wants a ready-made notice, with which it can build legally securely – and this guarantees the environmental impact assessment. The devil, however, sleeps at the border’, we are told at home. There is a lower limit for environmental impact assessment. And I come in or I don't come in, that makes a big difference in the cost. Therefore, thresholds are always an important point and a dispute point. This question is important for this: Do I need an environmental impact assessment? That's why I need to check if I get above the thresholds. And there is a procedure for finding out. This finding procedure is carried out very time-consumingly in some federal states, practically like an EIA. And we've now managed to get rid of this phase if an investor is sure he needs an EIA. Then there is no such finding at all on request. But so that it can't happen now that maybe who says it has been sloppy examined, selected NGOs mentioned in the law have been granted a special right that they can verify whether this finding procedure, which has resulted in the fact that an investor does not need EIA, has expired properly. This review procedure has no delaying effect, but gives civil society security that it fits. We have been able to prepare this in the law for a number of topics: in the hydropower, in the wind power, in the traffic. We now also have new provisions within them concerning airports subject to EIA, provisions that enforce the promises made to people in mediation proceedings, provisions that ensure that it becomes binding so that people can also get what has been promised to them. That too is a good story. A point that is very dear to my heart personally: After the shale gas has become a subject worldwide, the price of natural gas has fallen to a third in America, the industry is highly interested in promoting it. In Austria – that is what I allow myself to say – the OMV prepared this topic in a dilettant manner in the region. Instead of first looking at the idea of getting a proper procedure to work up the subject with people in such a way that no one has to fear, one has started a theatre that was incredible! That is why I am very pleased that we have quickly and in time – and I thank all the colleagues who have worked constructively – for shale gas in exploration, and in the promotion anyway, have added a separate category to the EIA so that this issue is also regulated and dealt with. [[Call the ÖVP:UVP subject!]] [[Further calls to ÖVP and Greens.]] That's right, it's subject to EIA! That is the same, and that is what we want, because in Austria we have a high standard of environmental legislation and therefore will only make an investment possible if it can be properly implemented. [[Abg. May. Brunner: That means we only know how it is approved!]] If this is not proven, it will never exist. I think those who come with the lot of money will also be interested in doing it properly. And then, when it is done properly, not even the green colleagues will have something against it. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, my speaking time is exhausted; not the subject, you will all say something about it. I quit for the first time and am really glad that we have implemented this EIA law. Mr. Federal Minister, congratulations, thank you very much!

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e28988	Mr President. Mrs Governmentary colleague! Ladies and gentlemen! In the Environmental Impact Assessment Act, the interests are interconnected, on the one hand, with those of the economy that wants approvals, on the other hand the interests of citizens, citizens' initiatives and neighbors. My particular task is to pool these interests and find a proper way that everyone can exist. With the present EIA legislation, we achieve faster, simpler, clearer procedures – that is the goal – while maintaining high environmental standards. On the one hand, the novelle brings improvements for people, for the environment, for the economy, it brings faster, unbureaucratic procedures, a faster clarity for all those involved, and we also create more civil rights. The law aims to ensure that ecology is compatible with economy, that environmental interests are carried out together with economic interests. For environmental and climate protection, we need the expansion of renewable energies. We also want to create the necessary framework conditions to remove stones. If we consistently reject nuclear power, move away from fossil energy sources, expand renewable energy sources, then we also need efficient approval procedures. We've already achieved a lot. Due to the last major EIA-Novelle in 2009, there has been a significant reduction in the duration of the proceedings. At the time, we took several steps to simplify the procedure in Sillian, at the government clause in East Tyrol. I recall the conclusion of the investigation procedure. The head of authority can say I have all the information, I don't need any more information. Oral proceedings may not take place. There is a simplification in the turbine exchange. The effect we have achieved with this: In the past, there was a 15-month procedure for the approval procedures, now there are 11 months, i.e. a four-month reduction. You can find out about the current EIA report sent to the National Council. The novels will continue these efforts. People are right to say that the bureaucracy is expansive, the administration needs to be reduced. We save with this novel bureaucracy, we also save money and increase efficiency because we take practical experience into account. We have made a round table in the Ministry of Life with representatives of the economy, NGOs and other stakeholders to include their experiences. However, they must not ignore the fact that the approval procedures in the EIA Act are country matters, the first instance is country matters. It therefore very much depends on the countries how generously they provide experts and how efficiently the procedures are handled. An improvement for the environment: Lever gas is subject to EIA, also the test drilling. This is important for people in the affected regions. They followed the debate in the Weinviertel. People there are very worried. So that's going to be EIA-compulsory. Of course, the pursuits of energy autarchy in Austria are implemented by various laws, eco electricity law, climate protection law, now EIA law. The thermal renovation is important. We are consistently working on the energy independence of Austria, namely, an energy-autarchic Austria, if, for example, we now have clearer guidelines for the EIA obligations for medium-scale power plants or there is no EIA obligations in small wind turbines. Less bureaucracy: There is voluntary EIA instead of a case-by-case check, i.e. one saves itself a pre-examination procedure when the project promoter says I make an EIA voluntarily. We have simplifications in the submission of documents. Not all documents must be available immediately at the start of the project. However, we also have accelerations by abandoning party status and speeding up the procedure. And we also realize more civil rights. The NGOs are given the opportunity to review the applications in the investigation procedure. If you address the fact that neighbours or citizens do not have rights, this is not true, because the relevant laws, for example in the construction order, are very likely to take into account neighbouring rights in the trade regulations. In conclusion: The objective must be to promote joint projects, to promote economic development while respecting our high environmental standards. I would like to thank Mr Bures for having taken this common approach, with legislative instruments in my area of responsibility and in her field of responsibility, and I would like to thank Members of Parliament for their joint work. This is an important matter, where we can really make much progress in Austria, with ecological development, but also economic progress. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00164_d7e29611	Mr President. Estimated government members! Ladies and gentlemen! The environmental impact assessment is: a worldwide preventive environmental instrument. This innovation will further increase the efficiency of EIA procedures. Thus, the EIA law is constantly being developed and a necessary balance between ecology and economy is created and found. The amendment will speed up, simplify and clarify the implementation of planning and approval procedures, taking into account environmental and social needs in the future. Projects such as the expansion of hydropower and the so-called small wind power can therefore be implemented more easily. This will further strengthen the pioneering role of Austria, which has already been given, in terms of electricity from renewable energy. This novel is therefore a continuous development of the 2009 novel, which has brought substantial simplifications, for example, for flood protection projects. With this novelle, Austria has been able to make it even more attractive as a location and still pursue a sustainable environmental policy. Necessary conditions are created and unnecessary stones are thus removed accordingly. Investors are thus given the opportunity to reach a decision on their project at a reasonable time. This will allow for economic development while ensuring the high quality of environmental protection, climate protection and water protection. With an agreement to this amendment, all parliamentary groups are invited to assume their responsibilities in this area.
